<dl id="mwAcM"><dt id="mwAcQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coreference" title="Coreference" id="mwAcU">Coreference resolution</a></dt><dd id="mwAcY">Given a sentence or larger chunk of text, determine which words ("mentions") refer to the same objects ("entities"). <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaphora_resolution" title="Anaphora resolution" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAcc">Anaphora resolution</a> is a specific example of this task, and is specifically concerned with matching up <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pronoun" title="Pronoun" id="mwAcg">pronouns</a> with the nouns or names to which they refer. The more general task of coreference resolution also includes identifying so-called "bridging relationships" involving <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Referring_expression" title="Referring expression" id="mwAck">referring expressions</a>. For example, in a sentence such as "He entered John's house through the front door", "the front door" is a referring expression and the bridging relationship to be identified is the fact that the door being referred to is the front door of John's house (rather than of some other structure that might also be referred to).</dd>
<dt id="mwAco"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discourse_analysis" title="Discourse analysis" id="mwAcs">Discourse analysis</a></dt><dd id="mwAcw">This rubric includes several related tasks. One task is discourse parsing, i.e., identifying the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discourse" title="Discourse" id="mwAc0">discourse</a> structure of a connected text, i.e. the nature of the discourse relationships between sentences (e.g. elaboration, explanation, contrast). Another possible task is recognizing and classifying the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speech_act" title="Speech act" id="mwAc4">speech acts</a> in a chunk of text (e.g. yes–no question, content question, statement, assertion, etc.).</dd></dl>

<dl id="mwAc8"><dt id="mwAdA"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1349394836" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt122" id="mwAdE" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Template:Visible anchor/styles.css&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;visible anchor&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Visible_anchor&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Implicit semantic role labelling&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">.mw-parser-output .vanchor>:target~.vanchor-text{background-color:#ebf4ff}@media screen{html.skin-theme-clientpref-night .mw-parser-output .vanchor>:target~.vanchor-text{background-color:#0f4dc9}}@media screen and (prefers-color-scheme:dark){html.skin-theme-clientpref-os .mw-parser-output .vanchor>:target~.vanchor-text{background-color:#0f4dc9}}</style><span class="vanchor" about="#mwt122" id="mwAdI"><span id="Implicit_semantic_role_labelling"></span><span class="vanchor-text">Implicit semantic role labelling</span></span></dt>
<dd id="mwAdM">Given a single sentence, identify and disambiguate semantic predicates (e.g., verbal <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frame_semantics_(linguistics)" title="Frame semantics (linguistics)" id="mwAdQ">frames</a>) and their explicit semantic roles in the current sentence (see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Semantic_role_labelling" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwAdU">Semantic role labelling</a> above). Then, identify semantic roles that are not explicitly realized in the current sentence, classify them into arguments that are explicitly realized elsewhere in the text and those that are not specified, and resolve the former against the local text. A closely related task is zero anaphora resolution, i.e., the extension of coreference resolution to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-drop_language" title="Pro-drop language" id="mwAdY">pro-drop languages</a>.</dd></dl>

<dl id="mwAdc"><dt id="mwAdg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textual_entailment" title="Textual entailment" id="mwAdk">Recognizing textual entailment</a></dt><dd id="mwAdo">Given two text fragments, determine if one being true entails the other, entails the other's negation, or allows the other to be either true or false.<sup about="#mwt124" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-rte:11_36-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;rte:11&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-rte:11-36&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-rte:11-36" id="mwAds"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAd0">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAd4">]</span></span></a></sup></dd></dl>

<dl id="mwAd8"><dt id="mwAeA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topic_segmentation" title="Topic segmentation" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAeE">Topic segmentation</a> and recognition</dt>
<dd id="mwAeI">Given a chunk of text, separate it into segments each of which is devoted to a topic, and identify the topic of the segment.</dd></dl>

<dl id="mwAeM"><dt id="mwAeQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argument_mining" title="Argument mining" id="mwAeU">Argument mining</a></dt>
<dd id="mwAeY">The goal of argument mining is the automatic extraction and identification of argumentative structures from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_language" title="Natural language" id="mwAec">natural language</a> text with the aid of computer programs.<sup about="#mwt127" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-37" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-37&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-37" id="mwAeg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAek"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeo">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAes">]</span></span></a></sup> Such argumentative structures include the premise, conclusions, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argument_scheme" title="Argument scheme" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAew">argument scheme</a> and the relationship between the main and subsidiary argument, or the main and counter-argument within discourse.<sup about="#mwt130" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-38" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-38&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-38" id="mwAe0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAe4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAe8">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt133" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-39" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-39&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-39" id="mwAfE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfM">[</span>39<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfQ">]</span></span></a></sup></dd></dl>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwAfU" aria-labelledby="Higher-level_NLP_applications"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Higher-level_NLP_applications">Higher-level NLP applications</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Natural_language_processing&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Higher-level NLP applications"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAfY"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Use_of_Firefox'_'Translate_selection_to_English'_machine_translation_on_Commons_talk_meta_pages_02.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAfc"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Use_of_Firefox'_'Translate_selection_to_English'_machine_translation_on_Commons_talk_meta_pages_02.png" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Use_of_Firefox%27_%27Translate_selection_to_English%27_machine_translation_on_Commons_talk_meta_pages_02.png/250px-Use_of_Firefox%27_%27Translate_selection_to_English%27_machine_translation_on_Commons_talk_meta_pages_02.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Use_of_Firefox%27_%27Translate_selection_to_English%27_machine_translation_on_Commons_talk_meta_pages_02.png/500px-Use_of_Firefox%27_%27Translate_selection_to_English%27_machine_translation_on_Commons_talk_meta_pages_02.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="1103" data-file-height="634" data-file-type="bitmap" height="144" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAfg"></a><figcaption id="mwAfk">Machine translation in Firefox</figcaption></figure>
<dl id="mwAfo"><dt id="mwAfs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automatic_summarization" title="Automatic summarization" id="mwAfw">Automatic summarization</a> (text summarization)</dt><dd id="mwAf0">Produce a readable summary of a chunk of text.  Often used to provide summaries of the text of a known type, such as research papers, articles in the financial section of a newspaper.</dd>
<dt id="mwAf4"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1349394836" about="#mwt134" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAf8" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Template:Visible anchor/styles.css&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;visible anchor&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Visible_anchor&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Grammatical error correction&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span class="vanchor" about="#mwt134" id="mwAgA"><span id="Grammatical_error_correction"></span><span class="vanchor-text">Grammatical error correction</span></span></dt>
<dd id="mwAgE">Grammatical error detection and correction involves a great bandwidth of problems on all levels of linguistic analysis (phonology/orthography, morphology, syntax, semantics, pragmatics). Grammatical error correction is impactful since it affects hundreds of millions of people who use or acquire English as a second language. It has thus been subject to a number of shared tasks since 2011.<sup about="#mwt138" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-40" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-40&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-40" id="mwAgI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgQ">[</span>40<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgU">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt141" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-41" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-41&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-41" id="mwAgY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgg">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgk">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt144" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-42" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-42&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-42" id="mwAgo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgw">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAg0">]</span></span></a></sup> As far as orthography, morphology, syntax and certain aspects of semantics are concerned, and due to the development of powerful neural language models such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GPT-2" title="GPT-2" id="mwAg4">GPT-2</a>, this can now (2019) be considered a largely solved problem and is being marketed in various commercial applications.</dd>
<dt id="mwAg8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logic_translation" title="Logic translation" id="mwAhA">Logic translation</a></dt>
<dd id="mwAhE">Translate a text from a natural language into formal logic.</dd>
<dt id="mwAhI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_translation" title="Machine translation" id="mwAhM">Machine translation</a> (MT)</dt>
<dd id="mwAhQ">Automatically translate text from one human language to another.  This is one of the most difficult problems, and is a member of a class of problems colloquially termed "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI-complete" title="AI-complete" id="mwAhU">AI-complete</a>", i.e. requiring all of the different types of knowledge that humans possess (grammar, semantics, facts about the real world, etc.) to solve properly.</dd>
<dt id="mwAhY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_language_understanding" title="Natural language understanding" id="mwAhc">Natural language understanding</a> (NLU)</dt><dd id="mwAhg">Convert chunks of text into more formal representations such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First-order_logic" title="First-order logic" id="mwAhk">first-order logic</a> structures that are easier for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer" title="Computer" id="mwAho">computer</a> programs to manipulate. Natural language understanding involves the identification of the intended semantic from the multiple possible semantics that can be derived from a natural language expression which usually takes the form of organized notations of natural language concepts. Introduction and creation of language metamodels and ontologies are efficient but empirical solutions. An explicit formalization of natural language semantics without confusion with implicit assumptions such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closed-world_assumption" title="Closed-world assumption" id="mwAhs">closed-world assumption</a> (CWA) vs. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-world_assumption" title="Open-world assumption" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAhw">open-world assumption</a>, or subjective Yes/No vs. objective True/False is expected for the construction of a basis of semantics formalization.<sup about="#mwt147" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-43" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-43&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-43" id="mwAh0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAh4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAh8">[</span>43<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiA">]</span></span></a></sup></dd>
<dt id="mwAiE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_language_generation" title="Natural language generation" id="mwAiI">Natural language generation</a><span typeof="mw:Nowiki" id="mwAiM"> (NLG):</span></dt>
<dd id="mwAiQ">Convert information from computer databases or semantic intents into readable human language.</dd>
<dt id="mwAiU">Book generation</dt>
<dd id="mwAiY">Not an NLP task proper but an extension of natural language generation and other NLP tasks is the creation of full-fledged books. The first machine-generated book was created by a rule-based system in 1984 (Racter, <i id="mwAic">The policeman's beard is half-constructed</i>).<sup about="#mwt150" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-44" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-44&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-44" id="mwAig"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAik"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAio">[</span>44<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAis">]</span></span></a></sup> The first published work by a neural network was published in 2018, <i id="mwAiw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1_the_Road" title="1 the Road" id="mwAi0">1 the Road</a></i>, marketed as a novel, contains sixty million words. Both these systems are basically elaborate but nonsensical (semantics-free) <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_model" title="Language model" id="mwAi4">language models</a>. The first machine-generated science book was published in 2019 (Beta Writer, <i id="mwAi8">Lithium-Ion Batteries</i>, Springer, Cham).<sup about="#mwt153" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-45" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-45&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-45" id="mwAjA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjI">[</span>45<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjM">]</span></span></a></sup> Unlike <i id="mwAjQ">Racter</i> and <i id="mwAjU">1 the Road</i>, this is grounded on factual knowledge and based on text summarization.</dd>
<dt id="mwAjY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Document_AI" title="Document AI" id="mwAjc">Document AI</a></dt>
<dd id="mwAjg">A Document AI platform sits on top of the NLP technology enabling users with no prior experience of artificial intelligence, machine learning or NLP to quickly train a computer to extract the specific data they need from different document types. NLP-powered Document AI enables non-technical teams to quickly access information hidden in documents, for example, lawyers, business analysts and accountants.<sup about="#mwt157" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-46" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-46&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-46" id="mwAjk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjs">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjw">]</span></span></a></sup></dd>
<dt id="mwAj0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dialogue_system" title="Dialogue system" id="mwAj4">Dialogue management</a></dt>
<dd id="mwAj8">Computer systems intended to converse with a human.</dd>
<dt id="mwAkA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Question_answering" title="Question answering" id="mwAkE">Question answering</a></dt><dd id="mwAkI">Given a human-language question, determine its answer. Typical questions have a specific right answer (such as "What is the capital of Canada?"), but sometimes open-ended questions are also considered (such as "What is the meaning of life?").</dd>
<dt id="mwAkM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text-to-image_generation" title="Text-to-image generation" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAkQ">Text-to-image generation</a></dt><dd id="mwAkU">Given a description of an image, generate an image that matches the description.<sup about="#mwt160" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-47" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-47&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-47" id="mwAkY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkg">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkk">]</span></span></a></sup></dd>
<dt id="mwAko">Text-to-scene generation</dt><dd id="mwAks">Given a description of a scene, generate a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3D_model" title="3D model" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAkw">3D model</a> of the scene.<sup about="#mwt163" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-48" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-48&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-48" id="mwAk0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAk4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAk8">[</span>48<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt166" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-49" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-49&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-49" id="mwAlE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlM">[</span>49<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlQ">]</span></span></a></sup></dd>
<dt id="mwAlU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text-to-video_model" title="Text-to-video model" id="mwAlY">Text-to-video</a></dt><dd id="mwAlc">Given a description of a video, generate a video that matches the description.<sup about="#mwt169" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-50" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-50&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-50" id="mwAlg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlo">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAls">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt172" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-51" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-51&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-51" id="mwAlw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAl0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAl4">[</span>51<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAl8">]</span></span></a></sup></dd></dl>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwAmA" aria-labelledby="General_tendencies_and_(possible)_future_directions"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="General_tendencies_and_(possible)_future_directions"><span id="General_tendencies_and_.28possible.29_future_directions" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>General tendencies and (possible) future directions</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Natural_language_processing&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: General tendencies and (possible) future directions"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAmE">Based on long-standing trends in the field, it is possible to extrapolate future directions of NLP. As of 2020, three trends among the topics of the long-standing series of CoNLL Shared Tasks can be observed:<sup about="#mwt175" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-52" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-52&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-52" id="mwAmI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAmM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmQ">[</span>52<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<ul id="mwAmY"><li id="mwAmc">Interest in increasingly abstract, "cognitive" aspects of natural language (1999–2001: shallow parsing, 2002–03: named entity recognition, 2006–09/2017–18: dependency syntax, 2004–05/2008–09 semantic role labelling, 2011–12 coreference, 2015–16: discourse parsing, 2019: semantic parsing).</li>
<li id="mwAmg">Increasing interest in multilinguality, and, potentially, multimodality (English since 1999; Spanish, Dutch since 2002; German since 2003; Bulgarian, Danish, Japanese, Portuguese, Slovenian, Swedish, Turkish since 2006; Basque, Catalan, Chinese, Greek, Hungarian, Italian, Turkish since 2007; Czech since 2009; Arabic since 2012; 2017: 40+ languages; 2018: 60+/100+ languages)</li>
<li id="mwAmk">Elimination of symbolic representations (rule-based over supervised towards weakly supervised methods, representation learning and end-to-end systems)</li></ul>

<section data-mw-section-id="16" id="mwAmo" aria-labelledby="Cognition"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cognition">Cognition</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Natural_language_processing&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Cognition"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAms">Most higher-level NLP applications involve aspects that emulate intelligent behavior and apparent comprehension of natural language. More broadly speaking, the technical operationalization of increasingly advanced aspects of cognitive behavior represents one of the developmental trajectories of NLP(see trends among CoNLL shared tasks above).</p>

<p id="mwAmw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cognition" title="Cognition" id="mwAm0">Cognition</a> refers to "the mental action or process of acquiring knowledge and understanding through thought, experience, and the senses."<sup about="#mwt178" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-53" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-53&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-53" id="mwAm4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAm8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnA">[</span>53<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnE">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cognitive_science" title="Cognitive science" id="mwAnI">Cognitive science</a> is the interdisciplinary, scientific study of the mind and its processes.<sup about="#mwt181" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-54" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-54&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-54" id="mwAnM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnU">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnY">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cognitive_linguistics" title="Cognitive linguistics" id="mwAnc">Cognitive linguistics</a> is an interdisciplinary branch of linguistics, combining knowledge and research from both psychology and linguistics.<sup about="#mwt184" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-55" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-55&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-55" id="mwAng"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAno">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAns">]</span></span></a></sup> Especially during the age of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Symbolic_NLP_(1950s_–_early_1990s)" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwAnw">symbolic NLP</a>, the area of computational linguistics maintained strong ties with cognitive studies.</p>

<p id="mwAn0">As an example, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lakoff" title="George Lakoff" id="mwAn4">George Lakoff</a> offers a methodology to build natural language processing (NLP) algorithms through the perspective of cognitive science, along with the findings of cognitive linguistics,<sup about="#mwt187" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-56" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-56&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-56" id="mwAn8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAoA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoE">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoI">]</span></span></a></sup> with two defining aspects:</p>

<ol id="mwAoM"><li id="mwAoQ">Apply the theory of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conceptual_metaphor" title="Conceptual metaphor" id="mwAoU">conceptual metaphor</a>, explained by Lakoff as "the understanding of one idea, in terms of another" which provides an idea of the intent of the author.<sup about="#mwt190" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-57" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-57&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-57" id="mwAoY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAoc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAog">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAok">]</span></span></a></sup> For example, consider the English word <i id="mwAoo">big</i>. When used in a comparison ("That is a big tree"), the author intends to imply that the tree is <i id="mwAos">physically large</i> relative to other trees or the author's experience. When used metaphorically ("Tomorrow is a big day"), the author intends to imply <i id="mwAow">importance</i>. The intent behind other usages, like in "She is a big person", will remain somewhat ambiguous to a person and a cognitive NLP algorithm alike without additional information.</li>
<li id="mwAo0">Assign relative measures of meaning to a word, phrase, sentence or piece of text based on the information presented before and after the piece of text being analyzed, e.g., by means of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Probabilistic_context-free_grammar" title="Probabilistic context-free grammar" id="mwAo4">probabilistic context-free grammar</a> (PCFG). The mathematical equation for such algorithms is presented in <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://worldwide.espacenet.com/patent/search/family/055314712/publication/US9269353B1?q=pn%3DUS9269353" class="external text" id="mwAo8">US Patent 9269353</a>:<sup about="#mwt193" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-58" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-58&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-58" id="mwApA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApI">[</span>58<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApM">]</span></span></a></sup></li></ol>
<dl id="mwApQ"><dd id="mwApU"><dl id="mwApY"><dd id="mwApc"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt194" id="mwApg" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot; {RMM(token_N)}\n=\n{PMM(token_N)} \n\\times  \n\\frac{1}{2d}\n\\left (\\sum_{i=-d}^d {((PMM(token_{N})} \\times {PF(token_{N-i},token_N,token_{N+i}))_i}\\right )\n&quot;}}"><span class="mwe-math-mathml-inline mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle {RMM(token_{N})}={PMM(token_{N})}\times {\frac {1}{2d}}\left(\sum _{i=-d}^{d}{((PMM(token_{N})}\times {PF(token_{N-i},token_{N},token_{N+i}))_{i}}\right)}">
  <semantics>

    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle {RMM(token_{N})}={PMM(token_{N})}\times {\frac {1}{2d}}\left(\sum _{i=-d}^{d}{((PMM(token_{N})}\times {PF(token_{N-i},token_{N},token_{N+i}))_{i}}\right)}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/43ccadd794a4b84e20d1209997a463342e0dfbfe" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -3.171ex; width:104.427ex; height:7.509ex;" alt="{\displaystyle {RMM(token_{N})}={PMM(token_{N})}\times {\frac {1}{2d}}\left(\sum _{i=-d}^{d}{((PMM(token_{N})}\times {PF(token_{N-i},token_{N},token_{N+i}))_{i}}\right)}"></span></dd></dl></dd></dl>

<dl id="mwApk"><dd id="mwApo"><dl id="mwAps"><dd id="mwApw"><i id="mwAp0">Where</i>
<dl id="mwAp4"><dd id="mwAp8"><b id="mwAqA">RMM</b> is the relative measure of meaning</dd>
<dd id="mwAqE"><b id="mwAqI">token</b> is any block of text, sentence, phrase or word</dd>
<dd id="mwAqM"><b id="mwAqQ">N</b> is the number of tokens being analyzed</dd>
<dd id="mwAqU"><b id="mwAqY">PMM</b> is the probable measure of meaning based on a corpora</dd>
<dd id="mwAqc"><b id="mwAqg">d</b> is the non-zero location of the token along the sequence of <b id="mwAqk">N</b> tokens</dd>
<dd id="mwAqo"><b id="mwAqs">PF</b> is the probability function specific to a language</dd></dl></dd></dl></dd></dl>

<p id="mwAqw">Ties with cognitive linguistics are part of the historical heritage of NLP, but they have been less frequently addressed since the statistical turn during the 1990s. Nevertheless, approaches to develop cognitive models towards technically operationalizable frameworks have been pursued in the context of various frameworks, e.g., of cognitive grammar,<sup about="#mwt197" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-59" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-59&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-59" id="mwAq0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAq4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAq8">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArA">]</span></span></a></sup> functional grammar,<sup about="#mwt198" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-60" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-60&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-60" id="mwArE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArM">[</span>60<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArQ">]</span></span></a></sup> construction grammar,<sup about="#mwt201" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-61" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-61&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-61" id="mwArU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArc">[</span>61<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArg">]</span></span></a></sup> computational psycholinguistics and cognitive neuroscience (e.g., <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACT-R" title="ACT-R" id="mwArk">ACT-R</a>), however, with limited uptake in mainstream NLP (as measured by presence on major conferences<sup about="#mwt204" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-62" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-62&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-62" id="mwAro"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArw">[</span>62<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAr0">]</span></span></a></sup> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_for_Computational_Linguistics" title="Association for Computational Linguistics" id="mwAr4">ACL</a>). More recently, ideas of cognitive NLP have been revived as an approach to achieve <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explainable_artificial_intelligence" title="Explainable artificial intelligence" id="mwAr8">explainability</a>, e.g., under the notion of "cognitive AI".<sup about="#mwt207" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-63" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-63&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-63" id="mwAsA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsI">[</span>63<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsM">]</span></span></a></sup> Likewise, ideas of cognitive NLP are inherent to neural models <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multimodal_interaction" title="Multimodal interaction" id="mwAsQ">multimodal</a> NLP (although rarely made explicit)<sup about="#mwt210" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-64" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-64&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-64" id="mwAsU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsc">[</span>64<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsg">]</span></span></a></sup> and developments in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_intelligence" title="Artificial intelligence" id="mwAsk">artificial intelligence</a>, specifically tools and technologies using <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Large_language_model" title="Large language model" id="mwAso">large language model</a> approaches<sup about="#mwt213" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-65" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-65&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-65" id="mwAss"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAs0">[</span>65<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAs4">]</span></span></a></sup> and new directions in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_general_intelligence" title="Artificial general intelligence" id="mwAs8">artificial general intelligence</a> based on the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_energy_principle" title="Free energy principle" id="mwAtA">free energy principle</a><sup about="#mwt216" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-66" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-66&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-66" id="mwAtE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtM">[</span>66<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtQ">]</span></span></a></sup> by British neuroscientist and theoretician at University College London <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_J._Friston" title="Karl J. Friston" id="mwAtU">Karl J. Friston</a>.</p>

(1981, August). <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://www.ijcai.org/Proceedings/81-1/Papers/071.pdf" class="external text" id="mwAyg">Control of Inference: Role of Some Aspects of Discourse Structure-Centering</a>. <li about="#cite_note-8" id="cite_note-8" data-mw-footnote-number="8"><span class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwAyo"><a href="#cite_ref-8" rel="mw:referencedBy" id="mwAys" aria-label="Jump up" title="Jump up"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAyw">↑</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-8" class="mw-reference-text reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt33" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAy0" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;extsrc&quot;:&quot;&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Cite journal&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Cite_journal&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;last1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Guida&quot;},&quot;first1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;G.&quot;},&quot;last2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Mauri&quot;},&quot;first2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;G.&quot;},&quot;date&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;July 1986&quot;},&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Evaluation of natural language processing systems: Issues and approaches&quot;},&quot;journal&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Proceedings of the IEEE&quot;},&quot;volume&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;74&quot;},&quot;issue&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;7&quot;},&quot;pages&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1026–1035&quot;},&quot;doi&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;10.1109/PROC.1986.13580&quot;},&quot;bibcode&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1986IEEEP..74.1026G&quot;},&quot;s2cid&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;30688575&quot;},&quot;issn&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;1558-2256&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><cite id="CITEREFGuidaMauri1986" class="citation journal cs1" about="#mwt33">Guida, G.; Mauri, G. (July 1986). "Evaluation of natural language processing systems: Issues and approaches". <i id="mwAy4">Proceedings of the IEEE</i>. <b id="mwAy8">74</b> (7): <span class="nowrap" id="mwAzA">1026–</span>1035. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibcode_(identifier)" title="Bibcode (identifier)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAzE">Bibcode</a>:<a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/1986IEEEP..74.1026G" class="external text" id="mwAzI">1986IEEEP..74.1026G</a>. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_(identifier)" title="Doi (identifier)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAzM">doi</a>:<a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://doi.org/10.1109%2FPROC.1986.13580" class="external text" id="mwAzQ">10.1109/PROC.1986.13580</a>. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISSN_(identifier)" title="ISSN (identifier)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAzU">ISSN</a><span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwAzY">&nbsp;</span><a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://search.worldcat.org/issn/1558-2256" class="external text" id="mwAzc">1558-2256</a>. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S2CID_(identifier)" title="S2CID (identifier)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAzg">S2CID</a><span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwAzk">&nbsp;</span><a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://api.semanticscholar.org/CorpusID:30688575" class="external text" id="mwAzo">30688575</a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=article&amp;rft.jtitle=Proceedings+of+the+IEEE&amp;rft.atitle=Evaluation+of+natural+language+processing+systems%3A+Issues+and+approaches&amp;rft.volume=74&amp;rft.issue=7&amp;rft.pages=1026-1035&amp;rft.date=1986-07&amp;rft_id=info%3Adoi%2F10.1109%2FPROC.1986.13580&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fapi.semanticscholar.org%2FCorpusID%3A30688575%23id-name%3DS2CID&amp;rft.issn=1558-2256&amp;rft_id=info%3Abibcode%2F1986IEEEP..74.1026G&amp;rft.aulast=Guida&amp;rft.aufirst=G.&amp;rft.au=Mauri%2C+G.&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ANatural+language+processing" class="Z3988" about="#mwt33" id="mwAzs"></span></span></li>
<li about="#cite_note-9" id="cite_note-9" data-mw-footnote-number="9"><span class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwAzw"><a href="#cite_ref-9" rel="mw:referencedBy" id="mwAz0" aria-label="Jump up" title="Jump up"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAz4">↑</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-9" class="mw-reference-text reference-text">Chomskyan linguistics encourages the investigation of "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corner_case" title="Corner case" id="mwAz8">corner cases</a>" that stress the limits of its theoretical models (comparable to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathological_(mathematics)" title="Pathological (mathematics)" id="mwA0A">pathological</a> phenomena in mathematics), typically created using <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thought_experiment" title="Thought experiment" id="mwA0E">thought experiments</a>, rather than the systematic investigation of typical phenomena that occur in real-world data, as is the case in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corpus_linguistics" title="Corpus linguistics" id="mwA0I">corpus linguistics</a>.  The creation and use of such <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text_corpus" title="Text corpus" id="mwA0M">corpora</a> of real-world data is a fundamental part of machine-learning algorithms for natural language processing.  In addition, theoretical underpinnings of Chomskyan linguistics such as the so-called "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_of_the_stimulus" title="Poverty of the stimulus" id="mwA0Q">poverty of the stimulus</a>" argument entail that general learning algorithms, as are typically used in machine learning, cannot be successful in language processing.  As a result, the Chomskyan paradigm discouraged the application of such models to language processing.</span></li>

rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt49" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3I"
